# Creation, Grace & the Last Things · Reference Card

> Open book. This card is meant to be open while you work — including during
> quizzes. Nothing here is something you're expected to recall cold.

This course teaches what the Catholic Church holds about creation, the human person, original sin, grace, justification and the last things, the manuals' four treatises run as one story (creation, elevation, fall, grace, glory), from within the tradition and with every claim's weight marked. Its machinery is three habits: find the **act** behind a sentence and mark its level, no higher and no lower; keep what an act **defines or teaches** apart from how a **school explains** it, which is the two-column table every lesson's doctrine ends with; and state the hard facts plainly. Vatican I attached no anathema to the beginning of time; no clause of Trent's Session V says how original sin passes; Rome heard the *de auxiliis* case for nine years and declined to decide it; the Church has never said that any human being is damned, nor that none is; and a defined clause sends those who die with original sin only to hell while limbo stayed a hypothesis never defined. Other traditions are stated as their own confessions state them. The card holds the ladder as the course uses it and the level of every Church text the lessons cite, the course's defined-and-school discipline in one table, how to read the citations, the vocabulary, the positions and distinctions in the wording the lessons verified, the argument skeletons, the thinkers, what is assumed from sibling courses, and the traps. The ladder belongs to [`fundamental-theology` 4.4](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-04-the-ladder-of-doctrinal-authority.md); where that course and this card differ, that course wins.

## Authority and citations

### Levels of authority

**The ladder, as this course uses it.** Rung 1: proposed as divinely revealed, either solemnly defined or taught infallibly by the ordinary universal magisterium; owed faith. Rung 2: definitive, to be held ([dogmatic facts](#dogmatic-fact) sit here). Rung 3: authentic ordinary magisterium, owed religious submission of intellect and will. Then **the seam**: rungs 4 and 5 are not lower grades of a magisterial act but no magisterial act at all, graded by theologians. Rung 4: theologically certain or common teaching of the schools. Rung 5: freely disputed among Catholics. The procedure: find the **act**, not the book you read the sentence in; ask what the act proposes the claim **as**; read off the assent; and if the level is not manifest, **default down** (can. 749 §3: nothing is understood as infallibly defined unless that is manifestly established). Overstating and understating are both errors, graded strictly. The seam is crossable one way: when a pope deliberately judges a disputed matter it stops being a free question (HG §20), so a rung-3 act moves a claim off rung 5. Reading a genre's weight is [`fundamental-theology` 4.5](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-05-reading-a-documents-weight.md)'s; infallibility's conditions and dogmatic facts in general are [`fundamental-theology` 4.2](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-02-infallibility-and-its-conditions.md)'s.

**Phrases this card uses.**

- *Rung 1 by solemn judgment*: a canon, or a council's profession or decree sealed by an anathema or a *definimus*: *Firmiter* with Lateran IV's const. 3, Vienne's *definientes*, Lateran V, Florence's *diffinimus*, Trent's canons, *Dei Filius*'s canons. A council defines in its canons; its chapters expound ([chapters and canons](#chapters-and-canons)).
- *Its contradictory at rung 1*: a canon condemns a proposition in the sense it states, and what is defined is the contradictory of that proposition, no more. Whether a given text falls under the canon is a separate judgment ([application of a condemnation](#application-of-a-condemnation)). Reason clauses after a condemnation ("for …", "whereas …") are conciliar teaching, not each a definition [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md).
- *Rung 1 by the ordinary universal magisterium*: Scripture's plain statement, always proposed as revealed, with no definition needed: man made in God's image and Christ the Image [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md), the indwelling and adoption [4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md), predestination itself [5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md). Where such a reading is "defensible" but no act manifestly proposes the claim as revealed, default down: guardian angels [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md), immediate creation of the soul ("rung 1 when so argued", [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md)).
- *Rung 1 by confirmation and reception*: a local council's doctrine reaches rung 1 not by its rank but through Rome's confirmation or adoption and later definition: Carthage 418, Orange II [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md).
- *Rung 1 as worded*; *for what its words reach*: the "original sin only" clause [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md); Trent XXII can. 5 [6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md); *Firmiter* names the class of the reprobate without manifestly defining that it has members [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md).
- *Defines nothing of itself*: Michael VIII's profession at Lyons II (1274), a witness whose content may stand at rung 1 through other acts [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md).
- *Ex cathedra standing open*: *Benedictus Deus* (1336) says *diffinimus*, but whether it is *ex cathedra* is open ([`ecclesiology-and-mariology` 4.4](../ecclesiology-and-mariology/lessons/04-04-infallibility-in-exercise-and-the-ordinary-universal-magisterium.md) Ex 1); the content's rung does not wait on it, since Florence and the CDF's 1998 commentary carry it [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md).
- *At least rung 3*: a chapter clause no canon repeats; conciliar teaching that defined nothing new (Vatican II); a claim whose rung-1 reading is arguable but not manifest; the contrary of a proposition in a [global censure](#in-globo-censure). What it rules out is calling the claim freely disputed [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md).
- *Rung 3*: encyclicals and dicastery acts with ordinary papal approval. *Rung 3 at its lightest*: addresses, messages and audience catecheses (Paul VI 1966, John Paul II 1996, 1999 and 2000, Pius XII 1951). *Rung 3 for what it adds*: the *Credo of the People of God*, by its own account "not strictly a dogmatic definition" (§3). *Rung 3, a permission*: HG §36 on the body's origin.
- *Rung 4*: the schools' common teaching, theologically certain; *rung 4 at most*: a thesis with no act behind it [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md). A theologians' note lapses with their consensus: the lack of the vision for unbaptised infants was rung 4 until the mid-twentieth century, now rung 5 [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md).
- *Rung 5*: freely disputed; *a permitted school*: premotion, middle knowledge, Thomist efficacious grace [5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md), [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md). *Rung 5 inside two rung-3 limits*: the indwelling's school question once *Mystici Corporis* fenced it [4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md). *Left free by an act*: the days of Genesis as periods or a literary frame, rung 5, left free in 1909 [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md). A standing rung-3 act keeps a question off rung 5 even while its reason is disputed: monogenism [3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md).
- *Censure below heresy: default down*: "false, rash" is not "heretical" (*Cum occasione* prop. 5 as worded [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md); *Auctorem fidei* prop. 26 [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md)). Nor does a heresy formula settle a rung alone: Vienne also decreed a heretic's punishment for denying that usury is sinful, which [`fundamental-theology` 5.5](../fundamental-theology/lessons/05-05-the-loci-theologici.md) caps at "certain"; the verb, its object and reception decide [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md).
- *Rung 2*: definitive, not revealed; its denial is not heresy (can. 751) but sets one against definitive teaching (can. 750 §2): the [dogmatic fact](#dogmatic-fact) of 1656 [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md); a canonized saint is in glory [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md).
- *Locates, fixes no level*: the Catechism, each paragraph keeping the weight of what it summarizes; a catechism's pictures (the judgment-seat, "the fire of purgatory") are imagery [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md).
- *No magisterial level*: theologians, the Fathers and Aquinas as authors, the posthumous Supplement, the manuals (Pohle–Preuss, Ott), the International Theological Commission (its approval *in forma specifica* is its own ballots, not papal, [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md)), private colloquia, a pope's book or interview, other traditions' confessions. Weighed by their arguments.
- *No rung*, *no level*: an estimate of a science (John Paul II 1996 §4; Paul VI's "not demonstrated", 1966); a historians' judgment (Olivi as Vienne's target, which is no dogmatic fact, [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md)).
- *Law, no rung*; *discipline, no rung*: the Code's canons, the GIRM, the funeral rite, the cremation norms, Lateran V's preaching decree, the 1607 formula's precept, the 1611 ban, the formulary's oath, *In eminenti*'s ban on the *Augustinus*, the communion decrees of 1905 and 1910, the 1950 measures against de Lubac. Binding and changeable; no doctrinal rung.
- *Taught by no act*: that any named person is in hell [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md); that any human being is damned [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md). John XXII's sermons were no act [6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md). *Nothing defined*: purgatory's fire, place and duration [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md).

**The levels in this course, in one place.** Each lesson's own entry has the act; the links after each group name the lessons.

- **Rung 1.** One principle of all things; creation out of nothing of both orders, in their whole substance; freely, for God's glory; the beginning in time, through *Firmiter* [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md). No nature of evil; every creature good, matter and body included; the demons good by nature, evil of themselves; the married can reach beatitude; virginity better than marriage [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md). Angels exist, created, spiritual; the devil and demons punished for ever [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md). Every human being made in God's image; Christ the Image; free will not extinguished [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md). The rational soul of itself and essentially the form of the body [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md). The soul immortal, one for each body; the two-truths claim's denial [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md). Adam constituted in holiness and justice, lost for himself and for us; death the penalty of sin, not natural necessity; sin, not only death, passes to all; by propagation, not imitation, in each as his own; contracted, not committed; infants baptized for its remission; all that is truly sin taken away, concupiscence not truly sin in the reborn [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md). The just truly sin, lightly and daily [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md). Grace necessary for every salutary act, the beginning of faith included, and unowed to merit; not a mere facility; prevenient help needed and the moved will able to refuse; special help to persevere; not every work before justification a sin [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-what-grace-is.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md). Justifying grace and charity inhere, not favour alone; partakers of the divine nature and adopted sons, never one substance with God; the indwelling; grace lost by mortal sin and recovered by penance [4.1](lessons/04-01-what-grace-is.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md). Man can be raised above nature, not of himself [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md). Nothing before justification merits it; one formal cause in substance; certainty of grace not required [5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md). Trent's canons unrevoked [5.2](lessons/05-02-the-1999-joint-declaration.md). The justified truly merit increase of grace, eternal life and glory; hoping for the reward no sin [5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md). God predestines; no predestination to sin, God working evil only permissively; Christ died not for the predestined only; no one bound to believe his election; no certainty of perseverance without revelation [5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md). God knows future free acts, as [5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md) marks it. Grace can be resisted; the commandments possible to the just; freedom from necessity required for merit [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md). Each person judged at death, in one of three outcomes [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md). The vision of the essence at once, before the body; God incomprehensible; degrees of glory by merit; the communion of saints [6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md). Purgatory; the suffrages help; the purged reach heaven; a debt of temporal punishment can remain; the Mass offered for the dead [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md). Hell, perpetual; immediate descent of those dying in actual mortal sin, to unequal punishments; the lost lack the vision for ever [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md). All rise, just and unjust, with their own bodies which they now bear; the whole person rises [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md). Christ returns to judge all by their works [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md). Baptism necessary; those dying with original sin only go down to hell, punished unlike mortal sinners, as worded [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md).
- **Rung 2.** The five propositions are in the *Augustinus* in Jansen's sense [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md); a canonized saint is in glory [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md).
- **At least rung 3.** God created not to increase or acquire his beatitude [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md). Angels guard human beings [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md). Each soul immediately created (rung 1 when so argued); the denial of pre-existence [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md). The image survives sin; equal, indelible dignity [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md). Body and soul one nature, as worded; death the soul's separation from the body [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md). Integrity before the fall; integrity and immortality not owed to nature; the lost holiness supernatural; suffering entered by sin [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md). The concupiscence clause's reason, "of sin, and inclines to sin"; "injustice as their own" (VI ch. 3) [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md). Grace a stable, habitual gift; the Spirit himself given; charisms; Catechism divisions of grace [4.1](lessons/04-01-what-grace-is.md). Christ's power precedes, accompanies and follows good works (VI ch. 16) [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md). Faith, hope and charity "infused at once" [4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md). The supernatural unowed even to unfallen nature; the human vocation in fact divine [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md). "Alone" of the formal cause; faith the beginning, foundation and root; no certainty of faith that one has grace [5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md). Eternal life grace promised and reward [5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md). God wills each person saved; Christ died for every human being; no one predestined to hell; ch. 12's rule [5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md). Rejecting prop. 5 as worded; globally censured propositions (wording varies: see [in globo censure](#in-globo-censure)) [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md). One earthly life; no reincarnation, no second chance [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md). A light of glory needed; the saints' intercession and invocation; *Benedictus Deus*'s own precisions, regardless of its standing [6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md). "Principally" the Mass; the purged souls' own assurance [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md). The loss of God the chief punishment; a pain of sense; self-exclusion, no one there but by his own unrepented mortal sin [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md). The just rise glorified; the resurrection waits for Christ's coming [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md). A final trial and the Antichrist first; no one knows the day; the world renewed, not annihilated; the judgment public; GS 39's distinction [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md). Damnation a real possibility for every living person; in hope the Church prays for all [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md). Limbo no Pelagian fable; Innocent III's letter on the penalty of original sin [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md).
- **Rung 3.** Angels "pure spirits", persons with intellect and will; their sin unforgivable because irrevocable [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md). The body's origin may be researched, a permission; Scripture does not teach nature's inner constitution [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md). Intellect and freedom manifest the image; Christ restores it [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md). One principle of life, the rational soul [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md). A conscious "human self" subsists after death [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md). Contraries of globally censured propositions (1567, 1690, 1713), no precise note [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md). The faithful cannot embrace polygenism [3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md). The indwelling differs from heaven only by state, within two limits [4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md). God could create intellectual beings without calling them to the vision [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md). Conjugal acts "noble and worthy" [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md). The identical particles not required [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md). Mitigated millenarianism cannot safely be taught; *Laudato Si'* §243 [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md). The Church entrusts unbaptised infants to mercy and may hope; the limbo of the fathers [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md).
- **Rung 4.** *Simul* as "at once" [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md); evil as privation [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md); each person's own angel [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md); God as first cause, not rival [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md); three grades of the image [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md); no spirit generated; the separated soul an incomplete substance [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md); infused knowledge at most [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md); why the just sin daily [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md); original sin formally the privation of original justice, materially concupiscence [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md); grace a quality, a habit, and "actual grace" as a name [4.1](lessons/04-01-what-grace-is.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md); q.109's three helps [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md); the condign reading of can. 32, congruous merit, revival of merits, perseverance unmerited [5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md); grace infallibly bringing consent where God wills [5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md); the light a created habit, seeing God whole but not wholly, what the body adds [6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md); loss answering to aversion, sense to conversion [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md); the four gifts of the glorified body; Christ's rising as cause [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md); the Antichrist one man [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md).
- **Rung 5.** Whether the beginning can be proved [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md); each angel its own species, the choirs, the moment of the fall [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md); the days as periods or a frame [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md); the image in the mind alone [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md); unicity or plurality of forms [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md); when the soul is infused; whether reason proves immortality; whether I survive in the separated soul [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md); Adam created in grace or given it later [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md); how original sin passes [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md); how grace moves the will: premotion, concurrence, middle knowledge [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md); the order of uncreated and created grace [4.1](lessons/04-01-what-grace-is.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md); pure nature, and the natural desire's kind [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md); whether *Joint Declaration* §41 is right [5.2](lessons/05-02-the-1999-joint-declaration.md); election before or after foreseen merits; negative reprobation [5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md); how the saints know our prayers [6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md); fire, place and duration of purgatory [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md); where hell is, its fire [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md); what makes the risen body the same; the age of the risen [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md); how the world is transformed [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md); the hope that all are saved; how many are saved [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md); whether any infant dies with original sin only [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md).
- **Law, no rung.** Exorcism [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md); Lateran V's rules for teachers [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md); Ephesus's canons [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md); the 1950 measures [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md); the 1607 formula, 1611 ban, 1654 decree and *Dum praeterito* [5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md); the formulary's oath, *In eminenti*, the 1905 and 1910 decrees [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md); funeral canons and the GIRM [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md); Trent XXV's orders to bishops [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md); cremation norms [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md); the ban on fixing the day [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md); the funeral rite for unbaptised children [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md).

*Lessons:* every lesson marks levels: [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-what-grace-is.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-the-1999-joint-declaration.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md), [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md), [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md)

### Levels of authority used in this course

Every Church text the lessons cite, with the level the lessons mark, chronological. "Cited" means a lesson uses the text without assigning a rung; do not invent one. Scripture, the Fathers, the theologians and other traditions' confessions are in [Thinkers and texts](#thinkers-and-texts). No Denzinger numbers are printed ([citations](#citations-and-how-to-read-them)).

| Text | Date | Kind | Level as the lessons mark it | Lessons |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The creeds: the Apostles' Creed; the Creed of 381 (Western Latin form, Schaff) | undated; 381 | creeds | **rung 1**: the resurrection of the flesh; Christ's return to judge the living and the dead; the communion of saints; dualism denies the first article | [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| [Carthage](#council-of-carthage-418), plenary council of Africa, cann. 1–8 | 1 May 418 | African council | doctrine **rung 1**, not by the council's rank but by Zosimus's confirmation, Rome's adoption and Trent: can. 1 through Trent V can. 1 (3.1 fixes exactly what), can. 2 through V can. 4, can. 5 through VI can. 2, cann. 6–8 through VI can. 23. The "middle place" canon found in some manuscripts: doubtful, **no rung** | [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md) |
| Zosimus, [*Tractoria*](#tractoria) | summer 418 | papal circular (fragments) | a papal act on the route to reception; not separately graded | [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md) |
| Ephesus, cann. 1, 4; synodal letter to Celestine | 431 | ecumenical council | **law and discipline, no rung**; the letter receives a sentence on persons; defines nothing on grace | [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md) |
| [*Indiculus*](#indiculus) | c. 435–442 | Roman compilation, probably Prosper's (headed pseudo-Celestine in Denzinger) | **no magisterial level**: a witness to Rome's mind, weighing by reception; Trent VI ch. 16 adopts its sentence on merit | [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md) |
| Chalcedon, Definition | 451 | ecumenical council | **rung 1**: Christ of a rational soul and body | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [Athanasian Creed](#athanasian-creed) (*Quicumque*) | undated | creed of the Latin Church, not adopted in the East | **rung 1** for the eternity of punishment, with *Firmiter*; "all men shall rise again with their bodies", **rung 1**; quoted on soul and flesh as one man without a level | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md) |
| Anastasius II, *Bonum atque iucundum* | 23 Aug 498 | papal letter to the bishops of Gaul | a witness to immediate creation, part of the at-least-rung-3 case: parents hand on guilt and penalty, not the soul | [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md) |

| Justinian's edict to Menas, nine canons ([Origenist anathemas](#origenist-anathemas)), endorsed by the home synod of Constantinople | 543 | imperial edict and local synod | **no conciliar rung** of its own; cited as history (the demons' restoration, pre-existence, temporary punishment) | [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md) |
| Constantinople II: capitulum 11 naming Origen; the fifteen anathemas against Origen | 553 | ecumenical council; anathemas of uncertain authorship | capitulum 11 is the council's own act; the fifteen: standing contested, they add **no level** (can. 749 §3) | [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md) |

### Defined and taught, school and opinion

The course's characteristic discipline, collected from every lesson's two-column table: what an act defines or teaches, at its level, and the explanations that doctrine does **not** carry. A school's account can be common teaching (rung 4) or freely disputed (rung 5); either way, refuting it leaves the doctrine standing, and holding it adds nothing to the doctrine's level.

| Doctrine (defined or taught) | Level | What it does not carry: a school's explanation or an opinion | Lessons |
|---|---|---|---|
| One principle of all things; all made out of nothing, in their whole substance | **rung 1** | creation as a relation of dependence, not a change (ST I q.45 a.2 ad 2, a.3): theology | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| The world began, *ab initio temporis* | **rung 1** | that the beginning cannot be demonstrated (Aquinas) or can (Bonaventure): **rung 5** | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| Both orders made *simul* out of nothing | **rung 1** for "both, out of nothing" | *simul* as "at once", the angels made with the world: undefined, common teaching, **rung 4** | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md) |
| God created freely, free from all necessity; for his glory | **rung 1**; not out of need **at least rung 3** | how a free act squares with simplicity (ST I q.19 a.3); glory as goodness shown and shared, Bonaventure's gloss (CCC 293) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| No nature of evil; every creature good, matter and body included | **rung 1** | evil as the privation of a due good (Augustine; ST I q.48): **rung 4**; measure, form and order, and spirit ranked above body: a Father's analysis | [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md) |
| The demons good by nature, evil through themselves, punished for ever | **rung 1**; unforgivable because irrevocable **rung 3** | why and when they fell; first sin pride, envy after (q.63 a.2); fixed because an angel grasps all at once (q.64 a.2), or has no body to repent with (Damascene II.3) | [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md) |
| The married can reach beatitude, and virginity is better | **rung 1**; conjugal acts "noble and worthy" (GS 49) **rung 3** | Augustine's reserve about intercourse not sought for offspring: a Father's view | [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md) |
| Human death came through sin (Trent V can. 1) | **rung 1** | Wis 1:13 read of human death only, so the lion did not once live on herbs (ST I-II q.85 a.6; I q.96 a.1 ad 2); a cosmic fall read from Rom 8:20–22: **no act**, open | [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md) |

| The body's origin from pre-existing living matter may be researched | **rung 3**, a permission (HG §36) | that it happened, and how: science; "more than a hypothesis": an estimate, **no rung** | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |
| Scripture does not teach the inner constitution of nature | **rung 3** (PD §18) | the six days as periods or as a literary frame: **rung 5**, left free in 1909 | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |

| The image survives sin; free will survives | **at least rung 3**; free will **rung 1** | three grades of the image, nature, grace, glory (q.93 a.4): **rung 4** | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md) |

| Equal, indelible ontological dignity | **at least rung 3** | the personalistic norm: philosophy | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |

| One principle of life, the rational soul (1860) | **rung 3** | the rational soul "virtually contains" the lower souls (q.76 a.4): **rung 5** | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| Body and soul one nature, not two (GS 14; CCC 365) | **at least rung 3** as worded | one act of existing for soul and body (q.76 a.1 ad 5): **rung 5** | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |

| No one is there but by his own unrepented mortal sin; none lost but by his own fault | **at least rung 3** | why the will is fixed at death; only self-exclusion, or also a sentence: **rung 5**; whether grace in fact wins every will (a hope); Hart's will that cannot finally refuse (a theory) | [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md) |
| All the dead rise with their own bodies which they now bear; the whole person rises, not the soul alone (*Firmiter*; the creeds) | **rung 1** | what makes it the same body, particles reassembled (Athenagoras, Augustine), the soul with some same matter (SCG IV.81) or the soul alone (Durandus): **rung 5**; the separated soul no person: **rung 4** | [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md) |
| The identical particles are not required (DDF reply, 2023) | **rung 3** | that God will in fact return each particle to its first owner: permitted, **rung 5** | [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md) |
| The just rise glorified, like Christ (Phil 3:21; CCC 999; 1979, point 6) | **at least rung 3** | impassibility, brightness, agility, subtlety as four gifts: **rung 4** | [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md) |
| Christ returns to judge all by their works (the creeds; *Firmiter*) | **rung 1** | why judge twice (III q.59 a.5 ad 2) | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| A final trial and the Antichrist's deception come first (CCC 675–677) | **at least rung 3** | that the Antichrist is one man (III q.8 a.8): **rung 4**; naming him: nothing | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| No one knows the day (LG 48; GS 39); preachers may not fix it (Lateran V) | **at least rung 3**; the ban **law** | reckonings of the end from Daniel's or Revelation's numbers: no standing | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| Mitigated millenarianism cannot safely be taught (Holy Office, 1944) | **rung 3** | Augustine's thousand years as the Church's age: **rung 5** as exegesis | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| The world renewed, not annihilated (LG 48; GS 39) | **at least rung 3** | how, and which creatures it holds: **rung 5**; Aquinas's "no animals" (Suppl. q.91 a.5) a school opinion | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| God wills every human being saved | **at least rung 3** | antecedent and consequent will ([5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md)) | [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md) |
| No one knows, without revelation, whom God has chosen | **at least rung 3** | warnings as forecasts (Grisez–Ryan) or as real dangers (Balthasar): **rung 5** | [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md) |
| In hope the Church prays for all to be saved | **at least rung 3** | whether that licenses hope for all together, or only for each (Augustine): **rung 5** | [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md) |
| Baptism necessary; original sin each one's own (Trent) | **rung 1** | other remedies, God not being bound by his sacraments (ITC 2007) | [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md) |
| Those dying with original sin only go down to hell, punished unlike mortal sinners (Florence) | **rung 1** as worded | Augustine's mildest condemnation; Lombard's loss only | [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md) |
| Limbo no Pelagian fable (*Auctorem fidei*); the Church entrusts these children to mercy and may hope (1980; CCC 1261) | **at least rung 3**; the entrusting **rung 3** | Aquinas's natural happiness without grief; the ITC's hope | [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md) |
| That any infant dies with original sin only | **rung 5** | once common teaching, now one opinion | [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md) |

### Citations and how to read them

| Form | Points at |
|---|---|
| Trent, Session (Roman numeral), ch. *n*, can. *n* | a decree of the Council of Trent, numbered and quoted as in Waterworth's English (1848), which Schaff (*Creeds of Christendom* vol. 2) prints with the Latin. A chapter expounds; a canon defines ([chapters and canons](#chapters-and-canons)) |
| Session V, can. *n* | Session V has **no chapters**: a preamble, five numbered paragraphs, each a canon with its reasons, then a paragraph on Mary. Read the canon rule inside each paragraph. The Hanover College web transcription of Waterworth drops the closing penal sentence of can. 5's concupiscence declaration; Schaff prints it (Latin *Si quis autem contrarium senserit, anathema sit*) |
| Session VI, ch. *n*, can. *n* | a proem, sixteen chapters, thirty-three canons. The proem ("otherwise than as by this present decree is defined and declared") and ch. 16's close fence the chapters |
| VII, *On Baptism*, can. *n* | a separate series of canons inside Session VII |
| Session XXV, Decree concerning Purgatory | a decree with no canons |
| *Dei Filius* ch. *n*; can. *n* on God the Creator; can. II.3 | Vatican I's constitution: four chapters, each with its own series of canons ("can. II.3" is the third canon on revelation). Quoted in Archbishop Manning's English of 1871, the translation Schaff prints ("Schaff's translation" means Manning's); it has no word for *simul* |
| *Firmiter*, const. 1; const. 3 | Lateran IV's constitutions: const. 1 the profession of faith, const. 3 the anathema on every heresy against it |
| Carthage (418) can. *n* | the council's canons in the Latin numbering (can. 1 = African Code can. 109); the "middle place" canon appears in some manuscripts only |
| Orange II can. *n*; conclusion | twenty-five chapters (eight canons and seventeen sentences) and a closing profession |
| *Laetentur caeli*; *Cantate Domino* | Florence's Decree for the Greeks (1439) and Decree for the Jacobites (1442) |
| *Benedictus Deus*, first and second *diffinimus* | Benedict XII's constitution (1336): the vision, then the descent of the damned, then the general judgment (*nihilominus*) |
| ST I q.*n* a.*n*, *respondeo*, ad *n*; I-II; II-II; III | Aquinas, *Summa Theologiae*, by part, question, article and reply to an objection, quoted in the English Dominican Province translation (2nd ed., 1920) |
| Suppl. q.*n* a.*n*; Supplement, Appendix | the *Supplement*, compiled after Aquinas's death from his early *Sentences* commentary: not his mature view |
| SCG; *De malo*; II *Sent.* | *Summa contra Gentiles* by book and chapter; the disputed questions *On Evil*; the *Sentences* commentary by book and distinction |
| CCC *n* | a paragraph of the *Catechism of the Catholic Church* (1992; 1997). It locates and keeps the weight of its source; its English may pick a sense the act did not (CCC 327's "at once" for *simul*) |
| can. *n* | the 1983 Code of Canon Law: law, no rung. Can. 749 §3: nothing is infallibly defined unless manifestly so; can. 750 §2: definitive teaching; can. 751: heresy |
| HG §*n*; PD §*n*; DIM §*n*; MC §*n* | *Humani Generis*, *Providentissimus Deus*, *Divinum Illud Munus*, *Mystici Corporis*, by the vatican.va section numbers |
| LG *n*; GS *n* | *Lumen Gentium*; *Gaudium et Spes* |
| DF | *Dei Filius* |
| *Credo* §*n* | Paul VI's *Credo of the People of God* (1968) |
| CDF 1979, point *n* | the Letter on Certain Questions Concerning Eschatology |
| JD §*n*; OCS; Annex; Response | the *Joint Declaration on the Doctrine of Justification* by paragraph; the Official Common Statement and its Annex (1999); the 1998 Catholic Response |
| ITC §*n* | a paragraph of an International Theological Commission study (*Communion and Stewardship*, 2004; the 2007 study on infants) |
| DR; [DR *n*:*n*] | the Douay–Rheims (Challoner). A bracket gives the DR verse where its numbering differs: Wis 11:17 [DR 11:18]; Ps 4:6 [DR 4:7]; Mark 9:43–48 [DR 9:42–47]; Rev 20:10 [DR 20:9–10]; 1 Thess 4:15 [DR 4:14]. At 1 Cor 15:51 the Douay follows the Vulgate, not the Greek; its Sir 18:1 "created all things together" renders the Vulgate's *simul*, where the Greek has *koinē*, "in common" |
| ANF; NPNF1; NPNF2 | Schaff and Wace's *Ante-Nicene* and *Nicene and Post-Nicene Fathers*, public domain; their numbering can differ from modern editions (Cyprian's Ep. 51 in ANF is Ep. 55 in modern editions) |
| Schaff vol. 2; vol. 3 | *Creeds of Christendom*: vol. 2 the Latin creeds, Trent, *Dei Filius* and Mogila's *Orthodox Confession*; vol. 3 the Protestant confessions (Augsburg, the Formula of Concord) |
| *Apology* II, III, IV (*Concordia Triglotta*, 1921) | Melanchthon's *Apology of the Augsburg Confession*, cited by the Triglotta's article and paragraph numbers; the Triglotta's art. III ("Of Love and the Fulfilling of the Law") is numbered as part of art. IV in other editions |
| (lesson's translation) | the lesson's own rendering of a Latin or Italian original with no public-domain English: Carthage, Orange, *Firmiter*, Lyons II, Vienne, *Benedictus Deus*, Florence, Lateran V, the papal bulls and letters. Copyrighted English translations (Tanner, Deferrari, Neuner–Dupuis) locate only |
| (paraphrased) | a modern text under copyright (vatican.va English, the Catechism, the Joint Declaration), given in paraphrase or short phrases |
| No DH, DS or D numbers | the course prints no Denzinger numbers: a Denzinger–Hünermann number only where verified at a source that prints DH numbering, and the 1911 Denzinger's ("old D") numbers are not DH. A Denzinger number is an index, not a weight, and Denzinger's headings are its editors', not the council's ("Errors of Peter John Olivi"; "against the Albigensians"; the *Indiculus* headed pseudo-Celestine) |
| *in globo*; "respectively" (*respective*) | a list of censures applied to a list of propositions without pairing them: read no precise note off any one ([in globo censure](#in-globo-censure)) |
| FT, TS, T&G, S&L, MT, OT, AF, AMP, CHR *n.n* | sibling courses as the lessons abbreviate them: `fundamental-theology`, `thomistic-synthesis`, `trinity-and-god`, `sacraments-and-liturgy`, `moral-theology`, `old-testament`, `apologetics-foundations`, `ancient-medieval-philosophy`, `christology` |

*From* [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md); the citation conventions are [`fundamental-theology` 4.5](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-05-reading-a-documents-weight.md)'s

### Chapters and canons

A council defines in its canons; its chapters expound, and a chapter clause no canon repeats is the council's own solemn teaching, **at least rung 3** on that act alone.

**The rule for chapter text** (stated in [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) for *Dei Filius*; Trent's chapters get the same rule). *Dei Filius*'s canons carry its anathemas: they are where it defines. A chapter clause no canon repeats is **at least rung 3** on that act alone, and **rung 1** when you can name another act proposing the same content as revealed: an earlier definition, or the ordinary universal magisterium. Default down (can. 749 §3) keeps the course from leaning on the chapter's "believes and confesses". So the beginning in time is rung 1 through *Firmiter*, not through Vatican I, whose canon 5 has three clauses (out of nothing, freely, for God's glory) and no fourth; `trinity-and-god` 1.2's rung 1 for the divine attributes stands because *Firmiter* professes them; and "not to increase or acquire his beatitude" is at least rung 3, with credit for the argument for more (its denial contradicts *Firmiter*'s *incommutabilis*) [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md).

**In Trent.** "The canons are where the definition bites": what a canon condemns has its contradictory at **rung 1**, in the sense the canon states. The reasons that follow a condemnation ("for …", "whereas …") are conciliar teaching, not each a definition [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md).

- **Session V has no chapters.** The rule works inside each numbered paragraph. The concupiscence declaration is **rung 1**, since its penal clause condemns the contrary; whether that clause reaches the reason, "of sin, and inclines to sin", is not manifest, so the reason is **at least rung 3** (the footing [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md) gives integrity). Can. 4's added reason binds the sense its condemned clause states, a sin from Adam in all, infants included, not a rendering of *eph' hō* in Romans 5:12 [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md).
- **Session VI's chapters are fenced unusually strongly.** The proem forbids anyone to "believe, preach, or teach, otherwise than as by this present decree is defined and declared"; ch. 16 closes by calling the chapters "Catholic doctrine on Justification, which whoso receiveth not faithfully and firmly cannot be justified". That binds all to their doctrine without making every sentence, proof-texts and all, a definition. So a chapter claim no canon repeats is **at least rung 3**, and rung 1 only where its proposal as revealed is shown: ch. 7's "alone" (its substance rung 1 through cans. 10–11), ch. 8's faith as "beginning, foundation, root", ch. 9's no certainty of faith of one's own grace (cans. 13–14 rung 1), ch. 12's rule on predestination (its core rung 1 through can. 16), ch. 16 on merit; ch. 4's "or the desire thereof" is presupposed, **not rung 1** [5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md).

**Traps.** "Vatican I defined the beginning of time" gets the rung right and the act wrong ("ch. 1 with its canons" misnames it). Raising a chapter clause to rung 1 because the chapter "believes and confesses", or dropping it to an open question because no canon repeats it, are the two errors [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md); applied in [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-what-grace-is.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md)

### Application of a condemnation

A canon condemns a proposition in the sense it states; whether a given teaching falls under it is a separate question, about what that teaching asserts.

The canon's truth is defined, **rung 1**, and stands unrevoked (JD §42). Its application to a text is a judgment about that text's meaning. Think of a warrant with a description: withdrawing the warrant is one act; checking whether the person before you fits it is another. The *Joint Declaration* claims only the second: the warrant stands, but today's partner, as described in this text, "does not fall under" it (§41). That §41 is the Catholic Church's official judgment as a party is a fact, so "Rome never said that" is false; whether the judgment is *correct* is **freely disputed (rung 5)**, since no act proposes it to be believed, held or adhered to [5.2](lessons/05-02-the-1999-joint-declaration.md).

- **A sense, not a word.** Trent VI can. 9 condemns "faith alone" *in such wise as to mean* (*ita ut intelligat*) that nothing else cooperates and no preparation of the will is needed; an explanation that keeps cooperation does not assert the condemned proposition. Canons that condemn "X alone, to the exclusion of Y" (can. 11's "sole", "to the exclusion of", "only") are what the method's sentence form, "When Lutherans say X, they do not thereby deny Y", is aimed at [5.2](lessons/05-02-the-1999-joint-declaration.md).
- **The words can match while the claim does not.** Can. 24 condemns calling good works "merely the fruits and signs" of justification received; JD §39 says "the fruits and signs". A canon cannot define what another tradition's word names [5.2](lessons/05-02-the-1999-joint-declaration.md).
- **A requirement, not a feeling.** Can. 13 condemns making certainty of forgiveness "necessary" for obtaining it; JD §35 makes assurance no condition of forgiveness and concedes wavering. The canon stays **rung 1**; only its application to this text fails [5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md).
- **Trent names no one.** The decree condemns propositions, so whether a given Lutheran text falls under a canon is a further question [5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md).

**Traps.** "Rome revoked Trent in 1999" (no canon is revoked; an ecumenical agreement revokes nothing, [5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md)); "the condemnations were wrong"; "Rome never said §41" (it did, officially, as a party).

*Lessons:* [5.2](lessons/05-02-the-1999-joint-declaration.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md); set up in [5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md)

### Dogmatic fact

A fact not itself revealed but so tied to revealed doctrine that the Church must be able to settle it, and does settle it definitively: **rung 2**.

**The textbook case** [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md): that the five propositions are in Jansen's *Augustinus* and condemned in his sense. Alexander VII, *Ad sanctam beati Petri sedem* (16 October 1656), "declaramus et definimus" that the five were taken from the *Augustinus* and condemned "in the sense intended by" Jansen. *Regiminis apostolici* (15 February 1665) made the [formulary](#formulary) papal; its oath is law, no rung. Clement XI's *Vineam Domini Sabaoth* (16 July 1705) ruled that obedience is not satisfied "by that [respectful silence](#respectful-silence)": the condemned sense, which "their words plainly bear", must be rejected "not with the mouth only but with the heart". Defined in 1656, owed interior assent by 1705, and not revealed, so its denial is not heresy (can. 751) but sets one against definitive teaching (can. 750 §2) ([droit and fait](#droit-and-fait)).

- **A public text's sense, not a dead man's mind.** 1656's "sense intended by" Jansen and 1705's sense "their words plainly bear" are read as one, keeping the judgment on the book. Jansen died before it appeared and submitted it to the Holy See in his will; heresy is obstinate denial of what is proposed as revealed, so the fact says nothing of his soul and judges no other page [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md).
- **Canonization.** The CDF's 1998 *Doctrinal Commentary* (§11) lists canonizations among dogmatic facts: that a canonized saint is in glory is **rung 2**. There is no counterpart: the Church has never declared anyone damned [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md).
- **A contrast with no act.** That the proposition Vienne condemned was Peter John Olivi's is a historians' judgment, **no level**, and no dogmatic fact, since no act attributes the sense to him as one did to Jansen [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md).

**Traps.** Calling the fact rung 1 (it is not revealed, so its denial is not heresy); calling it "not infallible, owed only deference", which 1656's *definimus* and 1705's "with the heart" refute; treating silence as obedience [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md). The general theory of dogmatic facts is [`fundamental-theology` 4.2](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-02-infallibility-and-its-conditions.md)'s.

*Lessons:* [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md), [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md); canonization [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md)

### In globo censure

A condemnation of a list of propositions under a list of censures applied "respectively" (*respective*), without saying which censure fits which proposition.

Such an act binds Catholics to reject each proposition but fixes **no precise note** on any one. The contrary of each is binding: **rung 3** in [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md) and in [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md)'s levels paragraph, written **at least rung 3** in [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md)'s table, [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md), [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md) and [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md). On either wording no contradictory reads off above rung 3 (default down, can. 749 §3): calling its denial heresy overstates, calling the question open understates.

- **Leo X, [*Exsurge Domine*](#exsurge-domine)** (15 June 1520), forty-one propositions of Luther. Prop. 2 (that to deny sin remains in a child after baptism is to trample on Paul and Christ) was not thereby called heresy: the act that puts "concupiscence is not truly sin in the reborn" at rung 1 is Trent V can. 5 [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md). Prop. 38 (the souls in purgatory unsure of their salvation): its contradictory **at least rung 3**, 6.3's decision [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md).
- **Pius V, *Ex omnibus afflictionibus*** (1 October 1567), against [Baius](#baius), notes from heretical down to offensive: the gratuity of integrity and of paradisal immortality (props. 26, 78) is rung 3, not rung 1, and a manual's *fidei proximum* (Pohle–Preuss) is not a rung [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md); props. 3, 11, 21, 55 on the supernatural's gratuity [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md).
- **The Holy Office, 7 December 1690** (thirty-one propositions) and **Clement XI, [*Unigenitus*](#unigenitus-1713)** (8 September 1713, Quesnel's 101): "all works of unbelievers are sins" was condemned in 1567, 1690 and 1713, each time *in globo*, and no act has said which note it earns [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md); the 1690 list also caught the rigorists' propositions [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md).
- **A single censure over a list** works the same way: Vienne's *Ad nostrum* condemns the Beghards' proposition 5 among "errors", with no note on it, so its contradictory is **at least rung 3** [6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md). The Holy Office's decree of 1679 condemned a laxist proposition "at least scandalous", a global censure: **rung 3** [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md).

**Not every list is global.** *Cum occasione* (1653) gives each of its [five propositions](#five-propositions) its own note, and [*Auctorem fidei*](#auctorem-fidei) (1794) gives prop. 26 its own: "false, rash, injurious to the Catholic schools", not "heretical" [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md), [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md).

*Lessons:* [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md), [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md), [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md); also [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md)

## Terms

Technical vocabulary in first-appearance order across the course (the first lesson whose teaching uses the term). Greek and Hebrew are transliterated; Latin glosses follow the lessons' translations. A linked term has a full entry.

| Term | Means | First used |
|---|---|---|
| [*De Deo Creante*, *De Deo Elevante*, *De Gratia*, *De Novissimis*](#the-four-treatises) | the manuals' four treatises: creation, elevation to grace, grace, the last things | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [new creation; new heavens and new earth](#new-creation) | the world renewed, not annihilated | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [*unum universorum principium*](#firmiter) | "one principle of all things" (*Firmiter*): no second principle for matter | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| *simul* | "together": either "at once" (angels created with the world) or "alike" (both orders out of nothing); undefined | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [*ab initio temporis*](#beginning-in-time) | "from the beginning of time" (*Firmiter*; DF ch. 1): the world is not eternal | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [*utramque … creaturam*; *deinde humanam*](#firmiter) | "each creature", spiritual and corporeal; "then the human", constituted of spirit and body (*Firmiter*) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [*de nihilo*, *ex nihilo*](#creation-out-of-nothing) | "out of nothing": not out of any material and not out of God's substance | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [dualism](#dualism) | two first principles, a good and an evil one | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [Cathars](#cathars) | the dualists *Firmiter*'s first clause is read against; the text names no one | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| ["in their whole substance"](#creation-out-of-nothing) | DF can. 5: the matter itself is made, not only its shape | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [emanation](#emanation) | Aquinas's word for creation, all being flowing from the universal cause; condemned only as things flowing out of God's substance | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [*liberrimo consilio*](#freedom-of-creation) | "with absolute freedom of counsel" (DF ch. 1, Manning): God created under no necessity | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [*non ad augendam suam beatitudinem, nec ad acquirendam*](#glory-of-god-as-end) | "not for the increase or acquirement of his own happiness" (DF ch. 1) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [*in se et ex se beatissimus*](#glory-of-god-as-end) | supremely blessed in and from himself: God gains nothing by creating | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [eternalists](#beginning-in-time) | those who held the world eternal yet made by God: "from nothing" without "after nothing" | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| [canon; chapter](#chapters-and-canons) | a council's defining sentence, with its anathema; the expository text around it | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| rung | a level on the course's ladder of authority, 1 (revealed) to 5 (freely disputed); [levels of authority](#levels-of-authority) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| ordinary universal magisterium | the bishops' constant teaching together, which can propose a truth as revealed with no definition | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| default down | when an act's level is not manifest, take the lower (can. 749 §3) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md) |
| Septuagint (LXX) | the Greek Old Testament, which adds "God saw that it was good" at Gen 1:8 | [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md) |
| [privation](#evil-as-privation) | evil as the lack of a due good, not a nature | [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md) |
| [Hyle](#manichaeism) | in Augustine's report, Mani's evil principle forming bodies; also the ancients' formless matter, which is not evil | [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md) |
| [Priscillianists](#priscillianists) | the sect Braga I (561) condemned for calling the body's formation the devil's work | [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md) |
| [renunciation, not contempt](#renunciation-not-contempt) | giving up a good for a higher one, against condemning it as evil; the reason decides | [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md) |
| [measure, form and order](#evil-as-privation) | Augustine's generic goods in everything God makes; evil is their corruption | [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md) |
| [nine choirs](#nine-choirs) | seraphim, cherubim, thrones, dominations, virtues, powers, principalities, archangels, angels (Pseudo-Dionysius): free opinion | [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md) |
| [guardian angel](#guardian-angels) | an angel's care for a human being: guardianship of all at least rung 3, each person's own angel rung 4 | [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md) |
| [demythologizing](#demythologizing) | Bultmann's programme (1941) of reading the New Testament's spirits as myth | [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md) |
| [pure spirit](#pure-spirit) | "pure spirits" (*Credo* §8), "purely spiritual" (CCC 330): taught, rung 3; more than *Firmiter*'s "spiritual" | [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md) |
| [*ha-satan*](#fall-of-the-angels) | "the accuser", a title in Job's heavenly court, not yet a proper name | [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md) |
| [*poena perpetua*](#hell) | "perpetual punishment" (*Firmiter*): the final state | [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md) |
| [*apokatastasis*](#apokatastasis) | universal restoration, the devil included in Origen's form | [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md) |
| [spiritual matter](#pure-spirit) | the Franciscan composition of spirits, souls included, from matter and form | [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md) |
| exorcism | the Church's prayer to expel the devil: law and liturgical law, `sacraments-and-liturgy` 7.3's | [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md) |
| [fixity of the angelic will](#fall-of-the-angels) | an angel adheres "fixedly and immovably" to what it chooses, so its fall is irrevocable (ST I q.64 a.2) | [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md) |
| [the two questions](#two-questions-frame) | how things came to be (science) against from whom and for what (faith) | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |
| ["what sensibly appears"](#providentissimus-deus) | PD §18: the sacred writers described appearances, not nature's inner constitution | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |
| ["special creation of man"; "unity of the human race"](#biblical-commission-1909) | two facts the Biblical Commission (1909) said could not be doubted | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |
| [immediately (of the soul)](#immediate-creation-of-the-soul) | with no created cause between God and the soul; says nothing of when | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |
| [*non prohibet*; *retinere iubet*](#humani-generis) | HG §36's two verbs: "does not forbid" (research on the body's origin), "orders us to hold" (the soul's creation) | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |
| [monogenism; polygenism](#monogenism-and-polygenism) | descent of all from one first pair; many first humans, or true humans not from Adam | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |
| ["more than a hypothesis"](#john-paul-ii-1996-message) | John Paul II's 1996 estimate of evolution: an estimate of a science, no rung | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |
| ["ontological leap"](#john-paul-ii-1996-message) | John Paul II's phrase (1996 §6) for the soul's coming, which observation cannot reach | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |
| [original sin](#original-sin) | contracted, not committed: a state, not an act, in each as his own | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |
| primary and secondary causes | God as cause of every cause, acting through creatures, not as their rival | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |
| [*in forma specifica* (ITC)](#communion-and-stewardship) | for the ITC, its own approval of a text by written ballots, not papal approval | [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md) |
| [image of God](#image-of-god) | what man is made in (Gen 1:26–27): intellect and freedom manifest it; Christ is the Image | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| [three grades of the image](#three-grades-of-the-image) | natural aptitude, conformity of grace, likeness of glory (ST I q.93 a.4) | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| [habitual (sanctifying) grace](#sanctifying-grace) | a stable gift that makes the soul holy and pleasing to God | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| [beatific vision](#beatific-vision) | the immediate sight of the divine essence | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| [image of creation, of re-creation, of likeness](#three-grades-of-the-image) | the gloss's labels on Ps 4:6 [DR 4:7], which Aquinas cites | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| [*tselem*, *demut*](#image-and-likeness) | Hebrew "image" and "likeness" (Gen 1:26) | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| ["to the image" (*ad imaginem*)](#christ-the-image) | Aquinas: the preposition signifies approach from a distance; the Son is the Image, man is made to it | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| [image and trace (*vestigium*)](#image-and-trace) | an image represents by likeness in species, a trace by way of an effect (ST I q.93 a.6) | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| [ontological, moral, social, existential dignity](#four-senses-of-dignity) | *Dignitas Infinita*'s four senses; the first can never be lost | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| [the final Adam](#christ-the-image) | GS 22's title for Christ, who reveals man to himself | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| communion of persons | where the image shines forth (CCC 1702; GS 12, 24) | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| [stewardship](#stewardship) | dominion as a share in God's governance of creation (CCC 373) | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| [similitude](#image-and-likeness) | ANF's English for Irenaeus's "likeness" | [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md) |
| [form; to inform](#soul-as-form-of-the-body) | the principle that makes matter this kind of living thing; Vienne: the rational soul is the body's form | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [Clementines](#council-of-vienne) | the collection of Clement V's constitutions, Vienne's among them, published by John XXII (1317) | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [*per se*; *essentialiter*](#soul-as-form-of-the-body) | "of itself" (no form between soul and body) and "essentially" (by what the soul is, not by an operation) | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [*reprobamus*; *definientes*; *decernimus*](#council-of-vienne) | "we reject"; "defining"; "we decree" a penalty: Vienne's three verbs, of different force | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [Peter John Olivi](#peter-john-olivi) | the Franciscan (about 1248–1298) whose view Vienne's proposition matches, on a historians' judgment | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [unicity of substantial form](#unicity-and-plurality-of-forms) | Aquinas's thesis that the rational soul is the body's only substantial form: rung 5 | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [form of corporeity](#unicity-and-plurality-of-forms) | a lower substantial form making matter a body (Franciscans, Scotists); no life-principle | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [one principle of life](#one-principle-of-life) | Pius IX (1860): in man the rational soul alone gives life: rung 3 | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| *nefesh hayyah* | Gen 2:7, "a living being" (DR "a living soul"): often the whole living person | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [ensoulment](#time-of-ensoulment) | when the soul is infused: undefined, rung 5 | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [death](#death) | the separation of soul and body | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [*tanquam probabiliorem*](#council-of-vienne) | "as more probable": Vienne's baptism clause, which defines nothing | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [dogmatic fact](#dogmatic-fact) | a non-revealed fact so tied to doctrine that the Church settles it definitively: rung 2 | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [death by neurological criteria](#death) | the complete, irreversible cessation of all brain activity as the sign of death (John Paul II, 2000) | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |
| [*confitemur*](#council-of-vienne) | "we confess": Vienne's opening Christological profession, not itself the definition | [2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md) |

| *definimus*; *diffinimus* | "we define": the verb of a solemn definition | [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md) |

| [generationism](#generationism) | the child's soul produced from the parents' souls | [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md) |

| [propagation, not imitation](#propagation-not-imitation) | Trent V can. 3: Adam's sin passes by descent, not by copying | [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md) |
| [monopsychism](#monopsychism) | one intellect for all human beings (Averroes as the Latins read him) | [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md) |

| ["in each one as his own"](#propagation-not-imitation) | Trent V can. 3: original sin is each person's own (*inest unicuique proprium*) | [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md) |

| ["human self"](#separated-soul) | the CDF's 1979 phrase for what subsists after death | [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md) |

| [immortality of the soul](#immortality-of-the-soul) | the soul's survival of death by its nature, one soul for each body: rung 1 | [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md) |

| [gratuity of the supernatural](#gratuity-of-the-supernatural) | the vision unowed: to merit (rung 1) and to nature as such (at least rung 3) | [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md) |

| [transmission of original sin](#transmission-of-original-sin) | how Adam's sin reaches each person: undefined, rung 5 | [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md) |

| ["in no way apparent how" (*cum nequaquam appareat quomodo*)](#humani-generis) | HG §37's reason against polygenism: epistemic, not a conditional | [3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md) |

| [bottleneck](#population-genetics-and-the-first-pair) | a sharp, brief fall in a population's size | [3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md) |

| [*parousia*](#parousia) | "arrival", a ruler's state visit: Christ's return in glory | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| [Antichrist](#antichrist) | the supreme religious deception, a pseudo-messianism (CCC 675) | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| [*tempus praefixum*; *mentitos*](#supernae-maiestatis-praesidio) | Lateran V's "fixed time" of future evils, and its "they lied" of past date-setters | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| [pilgrim Church](#pilgrim-church) | the Church on earth, holy but imperfect, bearing this passing world's form (LG 48) | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| [the harvest](#last-judgment) | the end of the world, when the angels separate wheat from cockle (Matt 13:30, 39) | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| [final trial](#antichrist) | the trial that shakes many believers' faith before Christ returns (CCC 675) | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| [millenarianism (chiliasm)](#millenarianism) | a reign of Christ on earth before the end (Greek *chilioi*, thousand) | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| [mitigated millenarianism](#mitigated-millenarianism) | Christ coming visibly to rule on earth before the final judgment: "cannot safely be taught" (1944) | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| [*tuto doceri non posse*](#mitigated-millenarianism) | "cannot safely be taught": the 1944 note, below "heretical" | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| [secular messianism](#secular-messianism) | realizing the kingdom inside history by human or political means (CCC 676) | [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |
| [*a me piace pensare all'Inferno vuoto*](#hope-for-all) | "I like to think of hell as empty" (Francis, 2024): no magisterial level | [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md) |
| [hope for each, hope for all](#hope-for-each-and-for-all) | hope for every person one by one, against hope that all together are saved | [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md) |
| [hope for all](#hope-for-all) | hope and prayer for the salvation of all, asserting nothing | [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md) |

| [*at saepius*](#hope-for-all) | "but often" (LG 16): the sentence on those deceived by the evil one | [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md) |
| [minatory and predictive](#threat-and-prediction) | the warnings read as real threats, or as forecasts (Dulles's pair) | [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md) |

## Positions and distinctions

**Creation (Module 1)**

### The four treatises

The manuals' four treatises on God's work, run in this course as one story: creation, elevation, fall, grace, glory.

*De Deo Creante* (God the Creator), *De Deo Elevante* (God who raises the creature to grace), *De Gratia* (grace) and *De Novissimis* (the last things). The course runs them as one arc because the first already points at the last: *Dei Filius* can. 5 says the world was made for God's glory, so it was made *for* something, and the [new creation](#new-creation) of Module 7 is that end reached. Every later treatise presupposes a world that is God's from the ground up.

**Level:** a manual's division of the matter, **no magisterial level**; the hinge it rests on, that the world was made for God's glory, is **rung 1** ([glory of God as end](#glory-of-god-as-end)).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md)

### New creation

At the end God renews the created world, with the human race; he does not annihilate it.

*Lumen Gentium* 48: the whole world, with the human race, will be perfectly re-established in Christ, a renewal already irrevocably decreed; *Gaudium et Spes* 39: the shape of this world, deformed by sin, passes away, and God prepares a new dwelling and a new earth; CCC 1042-1047. Scripture (DR): Rom 8:21, "the creature also itself shall be delivered from the servitude of corruption"; 2 Pet 3:13; Rev 21:5, "Behold, I make all things new"; 1 Cor 7:31, "the fashion of this world passeth away". Irenaeus: "neither is the substance nor the essence of the creation annihilated" (*Against Heresies* V.36.1, Rambaut). How, and which creatures it holds, GS 39 says we do not know. Aquinas's view that plants and animals do not remain (Supplement q.91 a.5, a posthumous compilation) is a school opinion; *Laudato Si'* §243 (2015) pictures each creature transfigured in its place. 1.1 links it as the end that creation for God's glory points to.

**Level (7.2 decides):** renewal, not annihilation, **at least rung 3** (LG 48; GS 39); a rung-1 reading through the ordinary universal magisterium is arguable but not manifest, so default down. How, and which creatures: **rung 5**. *Laudato Si'* §243: **rung 3**.

**Trap:** reading 2 Pet 3:10 as teaching annihilation: for the Douay's "burnt up" (Vulgate *exurentur*) Westcott and Hort print "will be found"; the verse is unsettled, and renewal is taught.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) (owner)

### Creation out of nothing

Everything that is not God has its whole being from him: made from no material, not out of his own substance, with no helper and from no need.

*Firmiter* (Lateran IV, 1215): God "made ... each creature out of nothing (*utramque de nihilo condidit creaturam*), the spiritual and the corporeal" (lesson's translation); *utramque* means spirit too is made out of nothing, so the angels fall under it (1.3). *Dei Filius* can. 5 (Vatican I, 24 April 1870) condemns whoever does not confess that the world and all it contains, "both spiritual and material, have been, in their whole substance, produced by God out of nothing" (Schaff, Manning's translation).
