- three replies, by name: particles reassembled (Athenagoras VIII; Augustine's loan returned to the lender), the soul with enough of the same matter (SCG IV.81), the soul with any matter (Durandus), all **rung 5**;
- the reply that runs sameness through the soul as the body's form, not through the corpse ([`thomistic-synthesis` 5.3](../thomistic-synthesis/lessons/05-03-the-human-soul-in-the-system.md); [soul as form of the body](#soul-as-form-of-the-body));
- where survivalist and corruptionist part, and what the corruptionist must add ([`metaphysics` 6.5](../metaphysics/lessons/06-05-what-a-person-is.md); [separated soul](#separated-soul)).

**Celsus's older objection** (Origen, *Against Celsus* V.14, tr. Crombie), not reserved: (1) no rational soul wants a rotted body back; (2) a wholly corrupted body cannot return; (3) God does nothing contrary to reason; ∴ pleading omnipotence is "a most absurd refuge".

*Lessons:* [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md)

### Argument: the hope for all

**Outline only from the verdict on: Boss 7(d) asks you to state the strongest objection, name the crux and say what your verdict commits you to.** The frame 7.3 teaches:

The four data every Catholic position must hold:

1. God wills all to be saved (1 Tim 2:4). [**at least rung 3**; [universal salvific will](#universal-salvific-will)]
2. Hell is real and its punishment eternal. [**rung 1**: *Firmiter*; Athanasian Creed]
3. No one is lost except by his own fault. [**at least rung 3**; no predestination to sin, **rung 1**]
4. The warnings are real, addressed to Christ's hearers, in the future tense (Luke 13:23-24; Matt 25:46, *apeleusontai*).

Three positions: many are lost, and we know it (long dominant: Augustine's "many more"); [hope for all](#hope-for-all), asserting nothing (Balthasar); all will be saved, and we know it ([universalism](#universalism) asserted). The objectors to Balthasar, by name: Grisez and Ryan (2014), Dulles (2003), Martin (2012); his defence: a warning is true if the danger is real. The distinctions that carry the dispute: [hope for each and for all](#hope-for-each-and-for-all); [threat and prediction](#threat-and-prediction). Level of the hope itself: **rung 5**.

*Lessons:* [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md)

### Argument: Hart for universal salvation

*That All Shall Be Saved* (2019), paraphrased.

1. A God who creates freely out of nothing answers for every final end he permits.
2. A rational will is moved by its nature toward the Good, so a creature freed of all ignorance and compulsion cannot finally refuse God.

∴ All will be saved, as a conclusion and not a hope; *aiōnios* means "of the age". Levels: it contradicts hell's defined eternity, **rung 1**, as *apokatastasis* did; Hart writes as an Eastern Orthodox theologian, **no Catholic level**. Marilyn McCord Adams reached the same conclusion from God's defeat of horrendous evils ([`apologetics-foundations` 2.3](../apologetics-foundations/lessons/02-03-evil-and-hiddenness-the-replies-that-cost-something.md)).

*Lessons:* [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md)

### Argument: Aquinas on the unbaptised children

ST III q.52 a.7, whose two arguments 7.4 reads, and two reasons for the children's freedom from grief.

*Not delivered at the descent:*

1. Deliverance came through union with Christ's Passion by faith and love.
2. These children had no faith of their own, lacking the use of free will, and were cleansed neither by their parents' faith nor by any sacrament of faith.
3. The fathers were delivered by admission to the vision, "to which no one can come except through grace"; these children had no grace.

∴ Christ's descent did not deliver them, though it freed the fathers ([limbo of the fathers](#limbo-of-the-fathers)).

*Why no grief:* early (*Sentences* II d.33; the Supplement's Appendix), original sin's only penalty is the loss of what the gift was for, and "no wise man grieves for being unable to fly like a bird"; later (*De malo* q.5 a.3), only faith plants knowledge of that end, so they do not know what they lack.

Levels: that those dying with original sin only lack the vision, **rung 1** ([*solo originali* clause](#solo-originali-clause)); that any infant dies so, **rung 5**; that unbaptised infants lack the vision, common teaching (**rung 4**) until the mid-twentieth century, now **rung 5**; limbo no Pelagian fable, **at least rung 3** ([*Auctorem fidei*](#auctorem-fidei)). "Limbo" is absent from the Tertia Pars, not from Aquinas (I-II q.89 a.6) ([limbo](#limbo)).

*Lessons:* [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md)

### Argument: the ITC's route to hope

**Outline only: Boss 7(d) asks you to reconstruct it in numbered premises.** The lesson teaches it in prose; its steps, by section of the 2007 study ([ITC 2007](#itc-2007)):

- the method: argue from the better known, since these infants' fate is unrevealed (§§41, 79);
- God wills all saved and did not bind his power to the sacraments (§82, citing ST III q.64 a.7): the sacrament's necessity is not absolute, Christ's is;
- granted, those who die in original sin lack the vision; the question is whether these infants must die in it (§§37, 83);
- possible remedies: conformity to Christ in their death, the Holy Innocents' pattern, a direct gift like Mary's preservation (§§85-87);
- Christ's solidarity with all outranks Adam's (§§88-91; Rom 5:15-20; GS 22);
- the Church that supplies faith at the font might supply a desire beyond it (§98), and her funeral prayer is evidence (§100; [*lex orandi*](#lex-orandi));
- the conclusion: grounds for hope, not knowledge (§102); the duty to baptize stands (§103).

Levels: an advisory study, **no magisterial level**. On limbo it says the theory never entered the dogmatic definitions, though the Magisterium at times mentioned it in ordinary teaching until Vatican II: say *never defined*, not *never taught*.

*Lessons:* [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md)

## Thinkers and texts

Every Father, theologian, council, author and non-magisterial text the lessons name, in date order, with the thesis as the lessons use it. A council, pope or dicastery appears here by what it did in the story; the weight of its act is in [the levels table](#levels-of-authority-used-in-this-course). Every author as author, Fathers and Doctors included, carries **no magisterial level**: weigh the argument. Popes appear as authors only where a lesson quotes them in a genre that binds no one (a sermon, a book, an interview). Paraphrase every modern author; quote only what the lessons quote.

| Trent (1545-1563) | ecumenical | [Session V](#trent-session-v) (1546); [Session VI](#trent-session-vi) (1547); Sessions XXII, XXIV; [Session XXV](#trent-session-xxv) (1563) | original sin; justification, merit, certainty, perseverance; the Mass for the dead; purgatory restated and its curiosities kept from the pulpit | Modules 3 and 5 throughout; [4.1](lessons/04-01-what-grace-is.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md) |
| Synod of Pistoia (1786) | Jansenist diocesan synod | condemned by [*Auctorem fidei*](#auctorem-fidei) (1794) | called limbo a Pelagian fable | [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md) |
| Vatican I (1870); Vatican II (1962-1965) | ecumenical | [*Dei Filius*](#dei-filius); *Lumen Gentium*, *Gaudium et Spes* | creation's predicates, God's incomprehensibility, the supernatural end; the pilgrim Church, the person, the renewal of creation | [1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md) |

## Assumed, not taught here

Every row points at the course that teaches it; an assumption with nowhere to look it up is a bug. This course states the doctrine, assigns the level and cites the rest: the ladder, the Scripture as text, the Fathers as texts, the metaphysics, the sacraments and the argued disputes are other courses'.

## Pitfalls

Every lesson's Watch out, deduped and grouped by theme, with the sharper traps the lessons' problems turn on. Each line is the wrong belief, then the correction. The course grades a collapsed level strictly in both directions, so most traps come in pairs.

### Traps with levels

- **A school's explanation given the doctrine's level, or a doctrine demoted because its explanation is disputed.** The course's characteristic error, both ways: the devil's existence (**rung 1**) is not optional because his fall's motive is opinion; premotion is not doctrine because Aquinas is the Common Doctor. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md))*
- **"Only the canons bind; the chapters are commentary", or "every chapter sentence is defined".** A chapter clause no canon repeats is **at least rung 3**, and **rung 1** only when another act proposes the same content as revealed; Trent VI's closing fence binds the doctrine without making every proof-text a definition. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md))*
- **A global censure read as fixing each proposition's note, or as settling nothing.** *In globo* (1520, 1567, 1690, 1713): the contrary binds, with no precise note: **rung 3** on the censure alone (4.2; 5.6 and 6.3 write **at least rung 3**, since no note is fixed); "heresy" overstates, "open question" understates. *Auctorem fidei*'s prop. 26 is not *in globo*: it carries its own note. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md), [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md), [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md))*
- **The wrong act named.** Vatican I for the beginning of time (it is *Firmiter*); *Exsurge Domine* for concupiscence (it is Trent V can. 5); Lyons II or Trent XXV for purgatory (it is Florence, with Trent VI can. 30); Michael VIII's profession defines nothing of itself. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md), [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md))*
- **A local or provincial act read as the definition, or dismissed as merely local.** Quiercy, Valence, Braga, the 543 edict carry no conciliar rung; Orange II is **rung 1** through confirmation, reception and Trent, not by its own rank. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md))*
- **A claim put at the rung of the book you read it in.** *Gaudium et Spes* 14 restates **rung 1** content; the Catechism locates and raises nothing (CCC 605 does not make Quiercy a definition; CCC 2010 does not define condign merit). Find the act. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md))*
- **A manual's note taken for a rung.** "Strictly of faith", *fidei proximum*, Ott's notes report the schools; the course's rungs come from acts, and a theologians' note lapses with their consensus. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md), [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md))*
- **An advisory commission, an interview or a book read as teaching.** *Communion and Stewardship*'s "*in forma specifica*" means the ITC's own ballots; the 2007 study, *Crossing the Threshold of Hope* and the 2024 interview all carry **no magisterial level**. *([1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md), [7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md))*
- **Discipline read as doctrine, or as nothing.** The 1607 formula, the 1611 ban, the 1905 and 1910 decrees, cremation law, Lateran V's ban on date-setting: **law, no rung**, often resting on a doctrine that is taught. *([5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md), [5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md))*
- **A translation's choice read as the council's.** The Catechism's "at once" for *simul* picks a sense; the council did not. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md))*

### Traps on creation

- **"Out of nothing" read as a figure, as naming a material, or as "after nothing".** It strictly negates any material (q.45 a.1 ad 3), and dependence does not by itself give a beginning: that needs its own act, *Firmiter*. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md))*
- **Aquinas's indemonstrability made Church teaching, or the beginning demoted because its proof is disputed.** Demonstrability is **rung 5**; the beginning, **rung 1**. Nor does Genesis 1:1 by itself say "out of nothing". *([1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md))*
- **"Emanation" always the condemned word.** Aquinas calls creation "the emanation of all being from the universal cause"; *Dei Filius* can. 4 condemns emanating from the divine substance: *by* God, not *of* God. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md))*
- **"For his glory" read as divine self-interest.** The glory is perfection shown in giving; a God *beatissimus* gains nothing. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-creation-out-of-nothing.md))*
- **"Evil is a privation" called dogma, or a mere opinion among equals.** Florence defines only the negative half (**rung 1**); the positive analysis is common teaching (**rung 4**). And a privation is a real lack in a real thing: it can hurt. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md))*
- **"Vatican I defined the goodness of creation."** The acts are *Firmiter* (1215) and Florence's Decree for the Jacobites (1442). *([1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md))*
- **Fasting, celibacy or virginity taken to deny creation's goodness.** Renouncing a good for a better presupposes it is good; Braga condemns abstaining only as from something unclean, and Trent XXIV can. 10 defines virginity better than marriage. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md))*
- ***Firmiter* made to stand or fall with the historians' Cathars.** It names no one; the level is the act's, not the target's. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md))*
- **"The demons are evil by nature."** Created good in nature, evil of themselves (*Firmiter*, **rung 1**). *([1.2](lessons/01-02-and-god-saw-that-it-was-good.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md))*
- **The nine choirs, the motive of the fall, or the angels' way of knowing taught as Church teaching.** No act proposes them. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md))*
- **Everyone's guardian angel called defined, or a nursery story.** Common teaching, **rung 4**; that angels guard us, **at least rung 3**. "Pure spirit" is **rung 3**; only "spiritual" is **rung 1**. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md))*
- **"God cannot forgive the devil"; "the dead become angels"; hope for the devil's restoration.** CCC 393 puts the cause in the irrevocable choice, not a limit to mercy; *Firmiter* distinguishes the angelic from the human creature; the hope for all concerns human beings, and the devil's punishment is **rung 1**. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-the-angels.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md))*
- **"1996 made evolution Church teaching", or "1950 condemned it".** 1950 is a permission (**rung 3**), 1996 an estimate of a science (**no rung**); neither binds a Catholic to a biological theory or forbids one. *Pascendi*'s evolution is of dogma. *([1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md))*
- **The 1909 responses void because the Commission is now advisory, or binding as in 1909.** Both wrong: they bound when issued, were never revoked, and Response III's facts keep their weight through other acts. *([1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md))*
- **"The Church teaches an age of the universe"; "the soul's creation needs a gap in the fossil record".** No act teaches a timetable; the doctrine places no event in the biological record. *([1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md))*

### Traps on the person

- **"The Church has defined what the image is."** Only *that* every human bears it is **rung 1**; intellect and freedom as its signs, **rung 3**; the mind alone, a school thesis. Vienne defines the soul as form, not the image. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md))*
- **Indelible dignity called open because Aquinas let grave sin forfeit "the dignity of his manhood", or defined because it rests on Genesis.** A **rung-3** act has spoken (*Dignitas Infinita*), so it is not rung 5; none proposes it as revealed, so not rung 1. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md))*
- **"Image" said of Christ and of us in one sense; the image as a capacity test.** The Son *is* the Image, in the same nature; we are made *to* it, like the coin. The first grade is an aptitude every newborn has whole. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md))*
- **"Vienne made Aquinas's psychology dogma", or Vienne demoted for its vocabulary.** The soul the body's form of itself and essentially, **rung 1**; that it is the *only* form, **rung 5**; the baptism clause chose an opinion "as more probable". *([2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md))*
- **"The soul sits in the brain."** It informs every living part; the brain-death criterion treats the brain as evidence of integration, not as the soul's address. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md))*
- **"Vienne condemned Olivi"; "Olivi taught two souls".** The decree names no one; Olivi taught one soul whose intellective part informs no body. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-body-and-soul-one-substance.md))*
- **"The Church teaches the soul is created at conception."** "Immediately" excludes created causes, not a moment: the timing is **rung 5**. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md))*
- **Immediate creation called defined dogma, or an encyclical's passing opinion.** Nothing defined it; **at least rung 3** is the floor. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-creation-and-evolution-in-church-teaching.md))*
- **"Gift of the creator" read as making immortality supernatural.** It is natural to a subsistent form, a gift only as all existence is; nor is the soul a spark of God (Leo IX: created from nothing). *([2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md))*
- **"Saying immortality cannot be proved violates Lateran V"; "Lateran V condemned Pomponazzi".** The bull condemns asserting mortality true in philosophy, not doubting a proof; it is of 1513, his book of 1516, and it names no one. "Double truth" names a charge, not a school. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md))*
- **"Augustine taught traducianism"; "553 is the defining act against pre-existence".** He never decided; 553's standing is contested, and the rejection is **at least rung 3** on other grounds. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-the-soul-immediately-created-and-immortal.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md))*

### Traps on original sin

- **"Trent defined that Adam was created in grace."** It wrote *constitutus*, not *creatus*, so as not to decide: **rung 5**. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md))*
- **The preternatural gifts all dogma, or all decoration.** Death as penalty **rung 1**, integrity **at least rung 3**, infused knowledge no act; and none was owed (Baius condemned, contrary **rung 3**). *([3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md))*
- **"Adam could not die."** He was able not to die; immortality by nature is the condemned proposition. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md))*
- **"The Church denies fallen nature is corrupt"; "wounded, not destroyed" quoted as a council.** Aquinas writes "corrupt nature"; what is denied is *total* corruption and an extinguished will (Trent VI can. 5, **rung 1**). The formula summarizes Trent VI ch. 1, q.109 a.2 and CCC 405; the gloss behind it is the *Glossa*'s. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md))*
- **"First parents" written into what is defined.** Trent's canons say "the first man, Adam"; the plural is CCC 390's, and it is what 3.4 keeps open. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-original-justice-and-the-fall.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md))*
- **Orange II called an ecumenical council or a solemn papal definition, or "only local, so optional".** A provincial synod whose doctrine is **rung 1** through confirmation, reception and Trent; it has one anathema, in the conclusion, and Aquinas never cites it. *([3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md))*
- **"Semi-Pelagian" taken as an ancient party's name.** The label is sixteenth-century (the Formula of Concord), and the quarrel over Molina made it a Catholic charge. *([3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md))*
- **Orange's canons read flat as propositions.** Can. 22 read flat collides with Baius prop. 25 and Trent VI can. 7; each carries its doctrine in the sense the Church receives. *([3.2](lessons/03-02-pelagius-augustine-and-the-councils.md))*
- **"Trent defined how original sin passes, through the marital act."** That is Augustine's account; the decree names the route and stops, open by silence, not by a clause; every mechanism is **rung 5**. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md))*
- **"Propagation" read as a genetic defect.** What passes is a privation in the soul; propagation names descent. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md))*
- **"The privation of original justice is dogma", or "Trent left it all open".** No act defines what original sin formally is (**rung 4**); that sin, not only death, passes to all by descent and is each one's own is **rung 1**. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md))*
- **Session V read as all new and all against Luther; can. 4 read as binding the Latin *in quo*.** Cans. 1, 2 and 4 re-issue Orange and Carthage; the new text is cans. 3 and 5. Can. 4 binds the sense, not the rendering. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md))*
- **"Luther taught that baptism leaves the guilt."** The *Apology*: baptism removes the guilt; concupiscence remains and is truly sin, not imputed, which is can. 5's target. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-trent-session-v-the-definition.md))*
- **"Genetics has disproved Adam."** The data exclude only a pair who were our sole ancestors within roughly 500,000 years, on stated assumptions. *([3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md))*
- **Monogenism called defined, or free because undefined.** It rests on *Humani Generis* §37, **rung 3**: an act has spoken and stands. Its reason is epistemic ("in no way apparent how"), not a conditional and not a proof. *([3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md))*
- **"The Catechism adopted the sin of the world"; "the reinterpretations deny the fall".** CCC 408 uses John's phrase for the effects of original and personal sins, and CCC 404 keeps propagation; Rahner kept a first sin after 1967, having defended monogenism in 1954. Ask which clause a proposal strains. *([3.4](lessons/03-04-monogenism-polygenism-and-the-modern-reinterpretations.md))*

### Traps on grace

- **Two "sanctifying graces" run together.** The English Dominican "sanctifying grace" of q.111 a.1 renders *gratum faciens*, a division by purpose; CCC 2000's divides by duration, the habit against actual graces. *([4.1](lessons/04-01-what-grace-is.md))*
- **The Catechism's "actual grace" taken to make the scholastic division dogma, or transient helps a school invention.** Needed, refusable prevenient help is **rung 1** (VI cans. 3-4); the name and its subdivisions, **rung 4**. *([4.1](lessons/04-01-what-grace-is.md))*
- **"Grace is God's self-gift", so the created gift is optional; or "grace is a quality" is defined.** Inherence is **rung 1** (can. 11), and Trent never says *habitus* or *qualitas*; quality and habit are **rung 4**; only the order of gift and Giver is open. *([4.1](lessons/04-01-what-grace-is.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md))*
- **Further "kinds" of grace; a charism as a certificate of holiness.** Operating and cooperating, prevenient and subsequent name one grace by its effects; prophecy can exist without charity, and no one knows with the certainty of faith that he is in grace. *([4.1](lessons/04-01-what-grace-is.md))*
- **"Without me you can do nothing" read as leaving the unbeliever only evil.** That is the condemned error; Carthage read the verse of "the fruits of the commandments". *([4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md))*
- **Grace as a repair kit for the fall.** Unfallen Adam needed the First Mover and a strength above nature; the fall added healing. *([4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md))*
- **The rejection of Baius prop. 27 promoted to rung 1 through Trent VI can. 5 or 7; can. 22 or 23 cited for q.109 a.8.** Neither canon covers prop. 27 (**rung 3**); cann. 22-23 concern the justified, and a.8 is **rung 4**. *([4.2](lessons/04-02-the-necessity-of-grace.md))*
- **"Made God" called pious exaggeration or an Eastern import.** 2 Pet 1:4 says "partakers"; Aquinas, that God alone deifies; what is excluded is a share in God's substance (*Dei Filius* can. 3, **rung 1**). *([4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md))*
- **The Thomist indwelling made the Church's because *Mystici Corporis* §79 uses its formula.** §78 declines to censure other explanations; the Thomist account stays **rung 4**, its rivals **rung 5**; Pius XII condemned no theory. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md))*
- **The indwelling as omnipresence turned up, or as a dim vision.** God is in a stone as its cause and dwells in the just as the known in the knower; it differs from the vision "in degree or state". Perichoresis is another doctrine. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-sanctifying-grace-the-indwelling-and-divinization.md))*
- **Gratuity taken to mean only that sinners do not deserve heaven.** That is the first layer, and Baius held exactly it; the elevation was unowed before sin too, **at least rung 3**. *([4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md))*
- ***Humani Generis* said to define pure nature or condemn de Lubac.** It names no one and never uses the term; it teaches a thesis about God's freedom at **rung 3**; pure nature stays **rung 5**; the 1950 measures were discipline. *([4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md))*
- **A disputed desire taken to make a disputed gift; "beyond nature's powers" taken for "not owed".** That the vision is unowed is fixed (rungs 1 and 3); only the desire's kind is argued. A wage paid by another exceeds your powers and is still owed. *([4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md))*
- **Rahner's supernatural existential read as natural openness to God.** It is supernatural, owed to no one. *([4.4](lessons/04-04-nature-and-grace-the-gratuity-of-the-supernatural.md))*

### Traps on justification and merit

- **"Trent denied that faith justifies."** Chapter 8 says we are "justified by faith"; can. 9 condemns "faith alone" in a stated sense, and can. 12 faith as mere confidence. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md))*
- **Co-operation read as making Trent semi-Pelagian.** The assent of ch. 5 is to grace that comes first, and can. 3 makes the whole preparation grace-borne. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md))*
- **"The alone formal cause" read as aimed only at Protestants; "not that whereby He Himself is just" as drafted against Osiander.** "Alone" excluded double justice, a Catholic proposal; the phrase is Augustine's, and the Osiandrian storm broke after 1549. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md))*
- **"Trent condemned imputation as such"; "works play no part".** It condemns justification by *sole* imputation, to the exclusion of inherent grace and charity; works in grace increase justice (can. 24). *([5.1](lessons/05-01-trent-session-vi-justification.md))*
- **"1999 revoked Trent's anathemas."** §41 concerns their application; §42 keeps them as salutary warnings; the canons stay **rung 1**. *([5.2](lessons/05-02-the-1999-joint-declaration.md))*
- **The signing made §41 doctrine, or the Declaration a theologians' paper.** No teaching act, so neither **rung 1** nor **rung 3**, yet signed by the Church as a party; only §41's correctness is freely disputed. The 1998 Response was no veto. *([5.2](lessons/05-02-the-1999-joint-declaration.md))*
- **"Differentiated consensus" taken for the Declaration's term; "basic truths" for total agreement; matched words for matched claims.** The phrase is the ecumenists'; §§5 and 43 limit the claim; JD 39's "fruits and signs" is not can. 24's "merely the fruits and signs". *([5.2](lessons/05-02-the-1999-joint-declaration.md))*
- **Merit called a late concession or a pious way of speaking.** Defined (can. 32, **rung 1**) and professed at Lateran IV (1215), with gift and merit paired inside the canon. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md))*
- **Condign and congruous merit, merit for others, or the revival of merits called defined.** No act uses the words: **rung 4** at most. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md))*
- **"Condign" read as worth heaven by itself.** As from free will alone an act has only congruity; condign *merit* is not Christ's condign *satisfaction*. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md))*
- **"God crowns his own gifts" quoted as denying merit.** The short form is conditional, and Augustine's full sentence keeps "your good merits". *([5.3](lessons/05-03-merit.md))*

### Traps on predestination and the schools

- **"Double predestination" taken as the condemned formula.** Valence confessed a predestination of the impious to death; what Orange and Trent VI can. 17 exclude is predestination *to sin*, and to no grace. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md))*
- **Predestination before foreseen merits called doctrine because Aquinas held it, or Calvinism because Calvin did.** **Rung 5**: permitted, untaught, uncondemned. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md))*
- **"The Church teaches that few are saved", or that all are.** No act fixes the number; Augustine's "many more" and Aquinas's "minority" are theirs; universalism asserted collides with hell's eternity. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md))*
- **Can. 15 read as defining all of ch. 12; predestination read as making prayer pointless.** Can. 15 condemns only making certainty of election an obligation of faith (ch. 12, **at least rung 3**, its core **rung 1** via can. 16); prayer and works fall under predestination as the way it is carried out. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md))*
- **"Rome sided with one school in 1607, or quietly condemned Molina."** The formula decided nothing; in 1654 the Holy Office disowned the alleged bull; each school is **rung 5**, not a tolerated error. *([5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md))*
- **The open question taken to open its limits.** That grace can be resisted is **rung 1** (VI can. 4), whatever *de auxiliis* left open. *([5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md))*
- ***De auxiliis* confused with the predestination question.** The axes cross: Bellarmine and Suárez held middle knowledge and predestination before foreseen merits. *([5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-predestination.md))*
- **Molina's God "only watches"; "physical" premotion read as bodily or coercive.** God gives the grace and concurs; middle knowledge is how he knows. "Physical" means real, against merely moral. *([5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md))*
- **The 1607 ban read as only "do not call it heresy", or given a rung, or read as a forecast.** It forbids any theological note or censure; it is a decision not to define plus a precept (**law, no rung**); *Grave nimis* (1483) preceded a definition (1854), so a ban predicts nothing. *([5.5](lessons/05-05-the-de-auxiliis-controversy.md))*
- **"Rome condemned efficacious grace, Augustine or Thomism with Jansen."** It censured five propositions; the Thomists were never reprobated (*Dum praeterito*, 1748): **rung 5**. *([5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md))*
- **All five propositions called heretical as worded.** The fifth is heretical only in the sense that limits Christ's death to the predestined; as worded it is "false, rash, scandalous". *([5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md))*
- **The fact called mere history (Pascal), or dogma.** **Rung 2**: denial sets one against definitive teaching, not heresy, and respectful silence does not satisfy (1705). *([5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md))*
- **One *Unigenitus*; the 1690 decree called a bull; the 1905 and 1910 decrees called definitions.** Clement VI's (1343) teaches the treasury, Clement XI's (1713) condemns Quesnel; 1690 is a Holy Office decree; 1905 and 1910 are discipline. *([5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md))*
- **Prop. 3's condemnation read as settling the compatibilism debate.** The Church requires freedom from necessity for merit in the fallen state without defining what that freedom is. *([5.6](lessons/05-06-jansenism.md))*

### Traps on the last things

- **The immediacy called a medieval opinion because Hebrews 9:27 says only "after this".** The verse fixes "once"; Florence fixes "at once": **rung 1**, whatever is said of *Benedictus Deus*. *([6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md))*
- **"Particular judgment" taken for a defined courtroom; the last judgment for a retrial, or made redundant.** The outcome is defined; the judgment-seat is the Roman Catechism's picture. *Nihilominus* holds both judgments, "according to different respects", and the soul's verdict is never reopened. *([6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md))*
- **"We know she is in heaven"; "we know where he is".** As hope, yes; as knowledge, the first claims what only canonization claims (**rung 2**), and no act has ever applied the rule for the lost to anyone. *([6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md))*
- **Reincarnation, or a second chance, called freely disputed.** Excluded, **at least rung 3**; purgatory holds only those who die "in the love of God". *([6.1](lessons/06-01-death-and-the-particular-judgment.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md))*
- **"John XXII refutes infallibility", or his view was heresy in 1331.** A preacher's opinion on an undecided question, never defined, submitted to the Church; no act yet excluded it. *([6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md))*
- **"No creature mediating" read as ruling out a created light; Vienne said to have defined the light of glory.** It excludes a creature *as the object seen*; one global censure gives **at least rung 3**, what the light is stays **rung 4**, and "Thomist jargon" understates, for the term is the council's. *([6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md))*
- **"Heaven is equal for all"; the two bulls called *Benedictus Deus* confused.** Degrees of glory by merit, **rung 1**, without rivalry; Benedict XII's is of 1336, Pius IV's, confirming Trent, of 1564. *([6.2](lessons/06-02-heaven-and-the-beatific-vision.md))*
- **Purgatory as a second chance, or hell with an exit.** It is "entirely different from the punishment of the damned" and ends in heaven. *([6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md))*
- **The fire, the place or the duration called defined; or the purification made painless.** Florence says *poenae* and names no fire; Trent named nothing; the 1979 letter forbids both arbitrary pictures and emptying Scripture's images. *([6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md))*
- **The souls' assurance of salvation called defined.** *Exsurge* prop. 38 is censured *in globo*: **at least rung 3**; their reaching heaven is **rung 1**. *([6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md))*
- **One text made to carry purgatory alone; Gregory's *Dialogues* given a rung.** No verse carries it alone; a pope writing a dialogue, or quoted by CCC 1031, gives it no magisterial level. *([6.3](lessons/06-03-purgatory.md))*
- **Hell's fire called defined because the creed says "everlasting fire".** The creed takes Scripture's word; a pain beyond the loss is **at least rung 3**, its nature **rung 5**. *([6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md))*
- **Hell's eternity hung on 553; *Benedictus Deus* cited for it.** It rests on *Firmiter* and the Athanasian Creed (**rung 1**); *Benedictus Deus*'s hell clause has *mox* and *cruciantur*, not *perpetuam*. *([6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md))*
- **"The Catechism replaced punishment with self-exclusion"; "a state, not a place" (1999) made eternity negotiable.** CCC 1035 keeps "punishments"; the 1999 phrase says what hell is, not how long. *([6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md))*
- **"Gregory of Nyssa was condemned in 553."** He is never named, and the fifteen strike a restoration bound to pre-existence, which he rejected; *Firmiter* excludes his conclusion. *([6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md))*
- **Annihilationism answered with Lateran V alone; "she canonizes, so she could declare the damned".** An annihilationist who grants natural immortality escapes Lateran V, not *Firmiter*; no act has ever declared a human being damned. *([6.4](lessons/06-04-hell.md))*
- **The resurrection read as the soul's survival, with Wisdom 3 as its text; only the just rising.** Wisdom 3 is about souls; the article is bodily, and all rise, **rung 1**. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md))*
- **The four gifts called dogma because the Roman Catechism lists them.** It calls them the Fathers' inference: **rung 4**. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md))*
- **"Their own bodies" read as the same atoms, so that cremation endangers the resurrection.** The identical particles are not required (**rung 3**, 2023); cremation is **law, no rung**. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-the-resurrection-of-the-body.md))*
- **Millenarianism called heresy, or open because Irenaeus held it.** The 1944 decree is **rung 3**, "cannot safely be taught"; a Father's opinion has no magisterial level. *([7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md))*
- **"No one knows the day" taken as a pious caution; 2 Pet 3:10 read as annihilation.** The first is taught, **at least rung 3**, and the ban on preachers is law; renewal is taught, and the verse's last verb is textually unsettled. *([7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md))*
- **Aquinas's "no animals in the new creation" given as the Catholic answer.** It is the posthumous Supplement, a school opinion; *Laudato Si'* §243 leans the other way: **rung 5**. *([7.2](lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md))*
- **"Balthasar taught that hell is empty"; "the Church now teaches the hope for all".** He hoped and asserted nothing; the Church prays in hope for all (**at least rung 3**), while hope that all together are saved is **rung 5**. *([7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md))*
- **"An eternal hell certainly empty" graded heresy; a reading of Matt 25:46 graded rung 1 because Scripture is inerrant.** The first contradicts **at least rung 3** (no one knows), not a definition; the reading is **rung 5**. *([7.3](lessons/07-03-hope-for-all.md))*
- **"Florence defined limbo", or "merely medieval".** It defines a conditional about a state, naming no infant: **rung 1** as worded; whether any infant dies so, **rung 5**. *([7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md))*
- ***Auctorem fidei* read as making limbo Church teaching; "limbo was never taught".** It rejected the charge that limbo is Pelagian, a permission, **at least rung 3**; say never *defined*, since it was common teaching and at times mentioned in ordinary teaching. *([7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md))*
- **"Christ emptied limbo on Holy Saturday"; "Augustine invented limbo".** He freed the fathers, not the children; Augustine taught condemnation, if mild, and limbo is the later schools' softening. *([7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md))*
- **The Supplement taken for Aquinas's mature view; limbo equated with pure nature; a funeral taken as proof.** The Appendix is the early *Sentences* commentary, and *De malo* changes its reason; the ITC keeps Aquinas's limbo apart from later pure nature; the rite shows hope, not knowledge. *([7.4](lessons/07-04-limbo.md))*

---

## Conventions

- **One card per course**, covering every lesson; the linter checks that every lesson file is cited somewhere on it.
- **Intuition first**, as in the lessons: a plain-English line before any Latin, canon or article.
- **Levels exactly as the lessons mark them.** Where a lesson says "at least rung 3", "law, no rung" or "no magisterial level", the card says the same; it never rounds a level up or down. Where lessons differ, the owning lesson's mark stands.
- **Boss problems are reserved.** The arguments a Boss part asks you to build appear in outline only; the texts, levels and positions are here, the reconstructions are not.
- **Headings are anchors.** Renaming a `###` breaks inbound lesson links. Headings are ASCII only; Latin, Greek and diacritics live in the body.
- **Modern authors and post-1930 Church texts are paraphrased**, cited by work, year and, where verified, section; quotations come only from the public-domain texts the lessons quote; the course prints no Denzinger numbers.
